Adding much more to the net seemed to make it fail to train. Claude said it was stuck weights and 
I should switch to leaky ReLu, which I did. Much better-behaved on the fake data, so I tried it on the real data.
With a little poking, I got the following:

Perhaps the solution is to get better metrics. Maybe we need more than two, and maybe we should
be able to score something as "uncommitted" when we don't care what score it gets. Something like:

Coherent / broken
Orderly / chaotic
Interesting / boring

And if something is too "broken" or "chaotic", having a high "interesting" score is not worth anything.

---

Trying to "retreat" to much simpler recognition goals:
 * "interest" now means "not a boring box"
 * "symmetry", renamed to "coherence" now means "doesn't have random garbage everywhere"

The theory is that forcing the user to do something other than the easiest possible thing will 
kick them out of "mechanistic" mode and make them build things that they like.

I created some automatic data (different dimensions of boxed rooms for "interest", and 
modifications of the input by adding noise for "coherence").

Model params: 2439621. Training items: 64. Test items: 30
Parameters: Args { conv: "3/12", fc: "32,8,4,4", lr: 0.01, epochs: 20, seed: 42, show_scores: true, fake_data: false }
Final interest loss (t/v) 0.075 0.038
Final coherence loss (t/v) 0.136 0.147
